3110. JOSEPH WICKINS was indicted for embezzlement.
MR. DOANE conducted the Prosecution.
HENRY WITHERS . I keep a brewery at Camberwell-green. The prisoner was in my service as drayman for about two years, up to last May—he had 18 s. a week—it was hit duty to receive money on my account—he was to account to me on the same day when he came home, or, if late, it was booked the next day—he did not account to me for 4 s. 6 d. received from Clark on the 6th of May for a cask of porter, nor for 4 s. 6 d. received from Mrs. Chandler, about the 23rd of May—I had sent her an account by the prisoner of 1 l. 7 s.—it was all made out in items of 4 s. 6 d.—this is it—the first item is At. 6 d., he did not account to me for that—the prisoner did not account to me on the 1st of August for St. 6 d. received from Mr. or Mrs. Matthews, for a cask of beer.
Cross-examined by MR. BALLANTINE. Q. Tell me the date on which he left your employ? A. On the 2nd of Sept., that was the last day he worked for me—I think it was on the 23rd of Sept. I gave him into custody—he had not gone into the employ of a rival brewer—there is a person of the name of Osman in my neighbourhood—the prisoner told me he was in the employ of Mr. Bailey, a brewer, in Wyndham-road—he did not tell me he should go into the employ of Osman—he has never been three days without accounting—I made the entries in a book which the prisoner saw—I have the whole of my books here—I do not know that he ever kept any book—he had sometimes ten or twenty customers to go to in a day.
Q. In addition to his wages, was he entitled to a certain sum of money in the event of his obtaining new customers? A. No, that has been dropped above twelve months—I used to give him 1 s. a customer—I do not owe him any money—when he called at my house, about a fortnight after he left me, he left word that he took 4 s. 6 d. and got drunk with it—I went down to his wife, and after that he called upon me—he did not say, as I owed him 20 s. it
would be all right—he never said at any time that I owed him any money—I did not account with him last Christmas for any money—when I went down to his wife, I said he had been round with Bailey and Osman's cards to solicit my customers—I had had the cards handed to me from the customers when I went round—I think I had told the policeman of this charge before I had been to the prisoner's wife, but the policeman could not catch him—after I found he had taken up the hills, I went to his wife's to see him, and I told her of this, as I considered I had a right to do so—he had been in my service upwards of two years—it might be an hour or two after I went to his wife that he called upon me—he was taken into custody on the 23rd, which was about a week after.
MR. DOANE. Q. When you gave notice to the police, you had not discovered these three sums? A. No—I found he had received a great many bills that he is not charged with now, and that was what I went down about—he said he bad taken up three bills, but they are not the bills charged here.
CATHARINE MATTHEWS . I am the wife of William James Matthews, of Eaton-square, Pimlico. On the 1st of Aug. I paid the prisoner 3 s. 6 d.—this is the bill he brought—he put this receipt to it—it was for the only cask of beer he ever brought me.
CATHARINE CLARK . I am the wife of Joseph Clark, of Jewin-crescent, Aldersgate-street. On the 6th of May I paid the prisoner 4 s. 6 d.—this is the bill—he wrote this receipt at the bottom—it is for a cask of porter, the only one I had.
MARY ELIZABETH CHANDLER . I am the wife of Matthew Chandler. On the 23rd of May I paid the prisoner 4 s. 6 d. off this bill of 1 l. 7 s.
PATRICK DALEY ( police-constable P 246.) I received information, and found the prisoner in about two hours—I told him I took him for embezzling various stints of money—he said he believed there was a difference of 4 s. 6 d.
Cross-examined. Q. Have you known him? A. Yes, about four years, in respectable employ.
NOT GUILTY .
Before Edward Bullock, Esq.
[trialAccount t18431023-3111]
3111. THOMAS DICKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of Oct., 4 handkerchiefs, value 4 s.; and 1 stock, 3 s.; the goods of Thomas Brooks and another, his masters.
CHARLES JOHN BROOKS . I superintend the business of my brother, Thomas Brooks and another, linen-drapers, High-street, Borough; the prisoner was in their service. On the 7th of October I told him we had reason to suspect he had robbed the firm—he said he had not taken anything belonging to us—I and my brother went with him, and searched his drawers, which were by the door of his room—they are for the use of the young men—they were unlocked then—we found in the drawer his clothes, and amongst them a satin stock, which appeared to have been worn—he then wished us to terminate the search, by pushing in the drawer—my brother said, "No, not yet, we must look in your pockets"—we found these four cambric handker chiefs in his coat-pocket—my brother asked if it was his coat, and he said, "Yes"—I know these handkerchiefs, by their having the mark of the firm upon them, and the stock by the private mark—we sent for an officer, and gave him in charge—he might have said something, but I do not recollect it—he was not entitled to purchase things, without having them entered in the book—the books are not here—there is no entry of these—I know they had only been in our stock a week—other persons sell besides me, but they are not here.
Prisoner. On the Saturday before you asked me to go on an errand for you. I asked you to get me some handkerchiefs, and you got me three dozen. I took them on the Sunday, and on the Monday I brought them back, and paid you 13 s. 6 d. for half a dozen of them. I said I wanted a dozen very good ones, and I told you on Monday they were not good enough. I wanted to go on a week-day, and you said, "Would it not do on Sunday?" I said,"Yes." On the Saturday these things came in; I took four, put them in paper, and put them into my pocket. I believe I told the person to enter them. You knew that the people were friends, and I have had goods before, and paid you. Witness. That was a fortnight before, when he took some handkerchiefs to show to a customer—he had them entered on a memorandum—he brought nine of them back, and the money for the others.
COURT. Q. Could he ever receive goods without their being entered in a book? A. Never—we should give any man in charge if he took them out without—he could not receive them to take out to show a customer without their being entered, because we do not allow it—those that he says he had to show, he had on memorandum, and I knew of it—I believe they were entered—I did not see them entered.
Prisoner. Have I not been entrusted to fetch goods from the City, to take to customers, without any entry? did I not fetch a lot of carpets, and take them to Captain Hudson, and other customers? I believe I told them to enter these handkerchiefs down. Witness. I have asked every young man in the establishment, and they say you did not; he was never entrusted to go out with goods from the shop to show, without their being entered—we have three shops—he was only in one shop—if he had teen trusted by any other person in the shop, it would have come to my knowledge—we have only about five young men there.
JOHN COOK ( police-sergeant M 8.) I took the prisoner.
Prisoner. He knew I was going to take these handkerchiefs to a particular friend—the stock was paid for—it was bought for me, by a lady, at their other concern.
JURY TO CHARLES J. BROOKS . Q. Were the handkerchiefs in the coat he wore every day? A. No, it was his best coat—they were folded up just at they are in the shop—I do not in general send persons with goods on a Sunday, only this was a friend of bis, and I said if he was going up, he might take them—the first lot he took with my knowledge was two dozen on the Saturday—he brought part of them back on the Monday, and the money—he might have said they wanted to see others, but I had no knowledge that he meant to take others.
NOT GUILTY .
Before Mr. Common Sergeant.

3112. ANN LINDFORD was indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of Sept 1 bed, value 6 s.; 1 coal-scuttle, 1 s.; 4 towels, 3 s.; 2 sheets, 2 s.; 2 blankets, 2 s. 6 d.; 2 candlesticks, 1 s., 1 pair of bellows, 1 s.; 3 carpets. 1 s.; 2 saltcellars, 2 s.; I tumbler, 1 s.; 1 teapot, 1 s.; 3 wine glasses, 1 s.; 1 look-ing-glass, 1 s.; 3 saucepans, 2 s. 6 d.; 1 frying-pan, 1 s.; 1 set of fire-irons, 2 s.; 1 spice-box, 6 d.; 3 tea trays, 2 s. 6 d.; 4 cups, 1 s.; 4 saucers, 1 s.; 12 plates, 6 s.; 3 basons, 1 s.; 1 pitcher, 1 s. 6 d.; 3 jugs, it.; 3 dishes, 1 s. 6 d.; 1 ladle, 6 d.; and 1 tureen, 1 s.; the goods of George Gardner, her master.
HARRIET SARAH GARDNER . I am the wife of George Gardner, and live in Walcott-place, Lambeth. The prisoner was in my service—on the afternoon
of the 28th of Sept, I left home, and all my property was safe—on my return I missed the things mentioned—these are them—they are ours.
WILLIAM ETHERIDGE ( police-constable L 35.) I took the prisoner at her lodgings, and found a shirt and towel there—she said Mrs. Gardner gave them to her.
Prisoner. Q. Did you see me in the room? A. I saw you at the door of the house—I found four duplicates on you, and the things at the pawnbroker's that they relate to.
Prisoner. These things are my own.
MRS. GARDNER re-examined. I am sure they are mine—they are marked with my husband's initials, G. G.
Prisoner. They left me in the house, owing me 30 s., and never said they were coming back. I was there two days, and never took anything from them.
GUILTY .— Confined Three Months.

3115. WILLIAM LLOYD was indicted for uttering counterfeit coin.
MESSRS. SCRIVEN and DOANE conducted the Prosecution.
SARAH KNOWLES . I am the wife of Henry Knowles. The prisoner came on the 30th of August for a pint of porter—he paid me a bad half-crown—I examined it, and put it into the till—there was nothing else in the till but a sixpence and a fourpenny-piece—I afterwards went to the till, took out the half-crown, and showed it to my husband—I then took it up stairs, and put it into a box, which I kept locked—on the 11th of September he came for half a pint of sixpenny ale, and gave me a bad shilling—my husband went after him, but did not find him—I placed the shilling in a cup on the shelf, behind some bottles—he came again on the 16th of Sept., to see if I would let him have half a pint of porter on trust—he said he would come again, and I trusted him—he came in again, about half-past eleven o'clock, and threw down a penny to pay for the porter, then he threw down a bad shilling, and said, "Change, if you please"—I gave the shilling to my son, and told him to fetch a policeman—I told the prisoner my son was gone to get change, and he waited—my son brought Green, an officer, with him—I gave the officer the half-crown and the shilling I had received from the prisoner, and my son gave him the other shilling.
COURT. Q. How was it you did not stop him when he brought the shilling?
A. It looked so well, I could not tell it from a good one, when he laid it down, and I had no one there.
HENRY KNOWLES . I am the son of Sarah Knowles. I saw the prisoner come in on the 16th—he put down a shilling on the counter—my mother gave it to me—I went for the constable, and gave it him.
MARK GREEN ( police-sergeant L 2.) I took the prisoner on the 16th—I produce the two shillings and the half-crown.
MR. JOHN FIELD . I am inspector of coin to the Mint. These two shillings and the half-crown are all counterfeit.
Prisoner's Defence. I never tendered the half-crown; I declare I never gave her the two first pieces; I never was in the house before, and she said, "I consider you are the person who uttered it."
GUILTY .* Aged 43.— Confined One Year.
[trialAccount t18431023-3116]
3116. ELIZABETH NUGENT was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of Oct., 1 box, value 5 s.; 8 sovereigns, 1 shilling, 1 sixpence, 1 10 l., and 2 5 l. Bank notes, the property of James Joseph Holland, her master.
JAMES JOSEPH HOLLAND . Iam a dyer, and live in High-street, Newington.
The prisoner was my shopwoman—on the 22nd of Oct. I missed a box from a drawer in my sister's bedroom—there was a 10 l. note, two 5 l. notes, and—some gold in it, I am not certain how much—I think the box was kept locked—my sister kept the key of it—I do not know when it was safe—on the 24th I received the box from the prisoner—the notes were in it right, and, as far at we know, the gold was right.
Cross-examined by MR. DOANB. Q. Did you say to the prisoner, if she would give you an account of what she knew about this box, you would deal mercifully with her? A. Yes—I do not know so much about this as my sister—I do not live on the premises.

3117. JOHN HARWELL was indicted for obtaining money by false pretences.
HARRIET REED . I am servant to Mr. Henry Gooch, at Camberwell. I received this paper from the collector of the Vauxhall Water-works, for 1 l. 11 s. 6 d. due for water—the prisoner afterwards came—he said he had called for the water-rate—I asked if it was right—he said, "Yes, quite right"—I asked him to give me a receipt, or sign the bill—he said he could not write, bat he would put his cross—I believed he came from the collector, and had a right to come from him, for the rate, and I paid him the money on that belief, and he put his cross to this paper.
HENRY WALLER . I am the superintendant of the Vauxhall Water-works. Our collector is Mr. Benjamin Walmsley—the prisoner had no right whatever to call for this money—he is not connected with the Company.
Prisoner. I did not call for any rate for the water—it is for watering the roads that I have got the contract for.
GUILTY . Aged 25.— Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18431023-3118]
3118. ANN RAFTER was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of Oct., 1 sovereign, and 1 half-sovereign; the monies of Joshua Ewbanke, from his person.
JOSHUA EWBANKE . I am shopman to Mr. Ashton, of Blackroan-street. On the 2nd of Oct. I had been with some friends, and about half-past twelve o'clock at night I went into a public-house—the prisoner was there, and from fifteen to twenty more men and women—she asked me to treat her to a glass
of ale—I refused—she afterwards asked for oysters—I refused—she kept annoying me—the landlord said if she did not behave better, he would turn her out—I had three sovereigns and a half and some silver in my pocket—she still annoyed me, and the landlord turned her out, and told me to stop, which I did for a quarter of an hour—I then went out—she was standing against the door alone—she put her hand round me in an indecent manner, and put it into my pocket, which was not buttoned—I pushed her from me, and missed a sovereign and a half—I charged her with the robbery—she knocked at a door, and called Jem —a black man came out and spoke to her—a policeman came along, and I gave her in charge—my money was not found.
Cross-examined by MR. CROUCH. Q. Where had you come from? A. Mr. Deret's rope manufactory—I had supped there-—I had a glass of sherry and water—I drank nothing but water at supper—I only went to this one public-house—I felt my money safe after she was turned out—I said nothing to her when I came oat—she directly took hold of me, and put her hand into my pocket.
COURT. Q. Why not seize her hand? A. She did it so quick—I was quite sober.
JOHN CHEESEMAN ( policeman.) I searched the prisoner, but found only 11 d. on her.
NOT GUILTY .
ADJOURNED TO MONDAY, THE 27 TH OF NOVEMBER.
